					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">16th-century Bishop of Rochester</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/John_Fisher_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="John Fisher (disambiguation)">John Fisher (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: #ffa4a4;"><span style="font-size:80%">Styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">John Fisher</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg" class="image"><img alt="Coat of arms of Saint John Fisher.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg/200px-Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg.png" decoding="async" width="200" height="199" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg/300px-Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg/400px-Coat_of_arms_of_Saint_John_Fisher.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1138" data-file-height="1131" /></a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Eminence" class="mw-redirect" title="His Eminence">His Eminence</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Eminence</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Informal style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">Cardinal</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>John Fisher</b> (c. 19 October 1469 – 22 June 1535) was an <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">English Catholic</a> <a href="/wiki/Bishop_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop (Catholicism)">bishop</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">cardinal</a>, and theologian. Fisher was also an academic and <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_(education)" title="Chancellor (education)">Chancellor</a> of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cambridge" title="University of Cambridge">University of Cambridge</a>. He was canonized by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a>.
</p><p>Fisher was executed by order of <a href="/wiki/Henry_VIII" title="Henry VIII">Henry VIII</a> during the <a href="/wiki/English_Reformation" title="English Reformation">English Reformation</a> for refusing to accept him as the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_head_of_the_Church_of_England" class="mw-redirect" title="Supreme head of the Church of England">supreme head of the Church of England</a> and for upholding the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>'s doctrine of <a href="/wiki/Papal_supremacy" title="Papal supremacy">papal supremacy</a>. He was named a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">cardinal</a> shortly before his death. He is honoured as a <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyr</a> and saint by the Catholic Church. He shares his <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a> with <a href="/wiki/Thomas_More" title="Thomas More">Thomas More</a> on 22 June in the Catholic <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">calendar of saints</a> and on 6 July in that of the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">Church of England</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>John Fisher was born in <a href="/wiki/Beverley" title="Beverley">Beverley</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yorkshire" title="Yorkshire">Yorkshire</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-huddleston_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-huddleston-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> in 1469, the eldest son of Robert Fisher, a modestly prosperous merchant of Beverley, and Agnes, his wife. He was one of four children. His father died when John was eight. His mother remarried and had five more children by her second husband, William White. Fisher seems to have had close contacts with his extended family all his life. Fisher's early education was probably received in the school attached to the collegiate church in his home town.
</p><p>Fisher studied at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cambridge" title="University of Cambridge">University of Cambridge</a> from 1484, where at <a href="/wiki/Michaelhouse,_Cambridge" title="Michaelhouse, Cambridge">Michaelhouse</a> he came under the influence of <a href="/wiki/William_Melton_(clergyman)" class="mw-redirect" title="William Melton (clergyman)">William Melton</a>, a pastorally-minded theologian open to the new current of reform in studies arising from the <a href="/wiki/Renaissance" title="Renaissance">Renaissance</a>. Fisher earned a <a href="/wiki/Bachelor_of_Arts" title="Bachelor of Arts">Bachelor of Arts</a> degree in 1487 and in 1491 proceeded to a <a href="/wiki/Master_of_Arts_(Oxford,_Cambridge,_and_Dublin)" title="Master of Arts (Oxford, Cambridge, and Dublin)">Master of Arts</a> degree. Also in 1491 Fisher received a papal dispensation to enter the priesthood despite being under canonical age.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Fisher was ordained into the priesthood on 17 December 1491 – the same year that he was elected a <a href="/wiki/Fellow" title="Fellow">fellow</a> of his college.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> He was also made Vicar of <a href="/wiki/Northallerton" title="Northallerton">Northallerton</a>, Yorkshire. In 1494 he resigned his <a href="/wiki/Benefice" title="Benefice">benefice</a> to become <a href="/wiki/Proctor" title="Proctor">proctor</a> of the university and three years later was appointed master debater, about which date he also became chaplain and confessor to <a href="/wiki/Lady_Margaret_Beaufort" title="Lady Margaret Beaufort">Margaret Beaufort, Countess of Richmond and Derby</a>, mother of <a href="/wiki/Henry_VII_of_England" title="Henry VII of England">King Henry VII</a>. On 5 July 1501, he became a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_sacred_theology" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctor of sacred theology">doctor of sacred theology</a> and 10 days later was elected <a href="/wiki/List_of_Vice-Chancellors_of_the_University_of_Cambridge" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Vice-Chancellors of the University of Cambridge">Vice-Chancellor of the university</a>. Under Fisher's guidance, his patroness Lady Margaret founded <a href="/wiki/St_John%27s_College,_Cambridge" title="St John&#39;s College, Cambridge">St John's</a> and <a href="/wiki/Christ%27s_College,_Cambridge" title="Christ&#39;s College, Cambridge">Christ's Colleges</a> at Cambridge, and a Lady Margaret Professorship of Divinity at each of the two universities at <a href="/wiki/Lady_Margaret_Professor_of_Divinity" title="Lady Margaret Professor of Divinity">Oxford</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lady_Margaret%27s_Professor_of_Divinity" title="Lady Margaret&#39;s Professor of Divinity">Cambridge</a>, Fisher himself becoming the first occupant of the Cambridge chair. From 1505 to 1508 he was also the President of <a href="/wiki/Queens%27_College,_Cambridge" title="Queens&#39; College, Cambridge">Queens' College</a>. At the end of July 1516 he was at Cambridge for the opening of St John's College and consecrated the chapel.
</p><p>Fisher's strategy was to assemble funds and attract to Cambridge leading scholars from Europe, promoting the study not only of <a href="/wiki/Classical_Latin" title="Classical Latin">Classical Latin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Greek_literature" title="Greek literature">Greek</a> authors, but of <a href="/wiki/Hebrew_literature" title="Hebrew literature">Hebrew</a>. He placed great weight upon pastoral commitment, above all popular preaching by the endowed staff. Fisher's foundations were also dedicated to prayer for the dead, especially through <a href="/wiki/Chantry" title="Chantry">chantry</a> foundations. Fisher had a vision to which he dedicated all his personal resources and energies. A scholar and a priest, humble and conscientious, he managed despite occasional opposition to administer a whole university, one of only two in England. He conceived and saw through long-term projects.
</p><p>A stern and austere man, Fisher was known to place a human skull on the altar during Mass and on the table during meals.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg/220px-Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg/330px-Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg/440px-Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3082" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bishop_John_Fisher_MET_DP242652.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>John Fisher as a young man by Pietro Torrigiano.</div></div></div>
<p>Erasmus said of John Fisher: "He is the one man at this time who is incomparable for uprightness of life, for learning and for greatness of soul."<sup id="cite_ref-foley_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foley-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Bishop">Bishop</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Bishop">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>By <a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">papal bull</a> dated 14 October 1504, Fisher was appointed the <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Rochester" title="Bishop of Rochester">bishop of Rochester</a> at the personal insistence of Henry VII.<sup id="cite_ref-CatholicCulture_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CatholicCulture-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Rochester was then the poorest diocese in England and usually seen as a first step on an ecclesiastical career. Nonetheless, Fisher stayed there, presumably by his own choice, for the remaining 31 years of his life. At the same time, like any English bishop of his day, Fisher had certain state duties. In particular, he maintained a passionate interest in the University of Cambridge. In 1504 he was elected the university's chancellor. Re-elected annually for 10 years, Fisher ultimately received a lifetime appointment. At this date he is also said to have acted as tutor to the future king, <a href="/wiki/Henry_VIII" title="Henry VIII">Henry VIII</a>. As a preacher his reputation was so great that Fisher was appointed to preach the funeral oration for King Henry VII and the Lady Margaret, both of whom died in 1509, the texts being extant. Besides his share in the Lady Margaret's foundations, Fisher gave further proof of his zeal for learning by inducing <a href="/wiki/Erasmus" title="Erasmus">Erasmus</a> to visit Cambridge. The latter attributes it (<i>"Epistulae"</i> 6:2) to Fisher's protection that the study of Greek was allowed to proceed at Cambridge without the active molestation that it encountered at Oxford.<sup id="cite_ref-huddleston_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-huddleston-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite his fame and eloquence, it was not long before Fisher came into conflict with the new King, his former pupil. The dispute arose over funds left by the Lady Margaret, the King's grandmother, for financing foundations at Cambridge.
</p><p>In 1512 Fisher was nominated as one of the English representatives at the <a href="/wiki/Fifth_Council_of_the_Lateran" title="Fifth Council of the Lateran">Fifth Council of the Lateran</a>, then sitting, but his journey to Rome was postponed, and finally abandoned.<sup id="cite_ref-huddleston_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-huddleston-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Fisher has also been named, though without any real proof, as the true author of the royal treatise against <a href="/wiki/Martin_Luther" title="Martin Luther">Martin Luther</a> entitled <i>"Assertio septem sacramentorum"</i> (<i><a href="/wiki/Defence_of_the_Seven_Sacraments" title="Defence of the Seven Sacraments">Defence of the Seven Sacraments</a></i>), published in 1521, which won for King Henry VIII the title <i>"Fidei Defensor"</i> (<i><a href="/wiki/Fidei_defensor" class="mw-redirect" title="Fidei defensor">Defender of the Faith</a></i>). Prior to this date Fisher had denounced various <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformation#Corruption" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant Reformation">abuses in the church</a>, urging the need for disciplinary reforms. On about 11 February 1526, at the King's command, he preached a famous sermon against Luther at <a href="/wiki/St_Paul%27s_Cross" title="St Paul&#39;s Cross">St Paul's Cross</a>, the open-air pulpit outside <a href="/wiki/St_Paul%27s_Cathedral" title="St Paul&#39;s Cathedral">St Paul's Cathedral</a> in London. This was in the wake of numerous other controversial writings; the battle against heterodox teachings increasingly occupied Fisher's later years. In 1529 Fisher ordered the arrest of <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Hitton" title="Thomas Hitton">Thomas Hitton</a>, a follower of <a href="/wiki/William_Tyndale" title="William Tyndale">William Tyndale</a>, and subsequently interrogated him. Hitton was tortured and executed at the stake for heresy.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Defence_of_Catherine_of_Aragon">Defence of Catherine of Aragon</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Defence of Catherine of Aragon">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>When Henry tried to annul his marriage to <a href="/wiki/Catherine_of_Aragon" title="Catherine of Aragon">Catherine of Aragon</a>, Fisher became the Queen's chief supporter.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> As such, he appeared on the Queen's behalf in the legates' court, where he startled the audience by the directness of his language and by declaring that, like St <a href="/wiki/John_the_Baptist" title="John the Baptist">John the Baptist</a>, he was ready to die on behalf of the indissolubility of marriage.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Henry VIII, upon hearing this, grew so enraged by it that he composed a long Latin address to the legates in answer to the bishop's speech. Fisher's copy of this still exists, with his manuscript annotations in the margin which show how little he feared the royal anger.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The removal of the cause to Rome brought Fisher's personal involvement to an end, but the King never forgave him for what he had done.
</p>
<h2><span id="Henry.27s_attack_on_the_Church"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Henry's_attack_on_the_Church">Henry's attack on the Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Henry&#039;s attack on the Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In November 1529, the "Long Parliament" of Henry's reign began encroaching on the Catholic Church's prerogatives. Fisher, as a member of the upper house, the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Lords" title="House of Lords">House of Lords</a>, at once warned <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_England" title="Parliament of England">Parliament</a> that such acts could only end in the utter destruction of the Catholic Church in England. The <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_England" title="House of Commons of England">Commons</a>, through their speaker, complained to the King that Fisher had disparaged <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_England" title="Parliament of England">Parliament</a>, presumably with Henry prompting them behind the scenes. The opportunity was not lost. Henry summoned Fisher before him, demanding an explanation. This being given, Henry declared himself satisfied, leaving it to the Commons to declare that the explanation was inadequate, so that he appeared as a magnanimous sovereign, instead of Fisher's enemy.
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1062260506">.mw-parser-output .quotebox{background-color:#F9F9F9;border:1px solid #aaa;box-sizing:border-box;padding:10px;font-size:88%;max-width:100%}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft{margin:.5em 1.4em .8em 0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright{margin:.5em 0 .8em 1.4em}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.centered{overflow:hidden;position:relative;margin:.5em auto .8em auto}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft span,.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright span{font-style:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox>blockquote{margin:0;padding:0;border-left:0;font-family:inherit;font-size:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-title{background-color:#F9F9F9;text-align:center;font-size:110%;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote>:first-child{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote:last-child>:last-child{margin-bottom:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:before{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" “ ";vertical-align:-45%;line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:after{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" ” ";line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .left-aligned{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .right-aligned{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .center-aligned{text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quote-title,.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quotebox-quote{display:block}.mw-parser-output .quotebox cite{display:block;font-style:normal}@media screen and (max-width:640px){.mw-parser-output .quotebox{width:100%!important;margin:0 0 .8em!important;float:none!important}}</style><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style="width:25%; ;">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>This yere was a coke boylyd in a cauderne in <a href="/wiki/Smithfield,_London" title="Smithfield, London">Smythfeld</a> for he wolde a powsyned the bishop of Rochester Fycher with dyvers of hys servanttes, and he was lockyd in a chayne and pullyd up and downe with a <a href="/wiki/Gibbet" class="mw-redirect" title="Gibbet">gybbyt</a> at dyvers tymes tyll he was dede.
</p>
</blockquote>
<p><cite class="left-aligned" style=""><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1133582631">.mw-parser-output cite.citation{font-style:inherit;word-wrap:break-word}.mw-parser-output .citation q{quotes:"\"""\"""'""'"}.mw-parser-output .citation:target{background-color:rgba(0,127,255,0.133)}.mw-parser-output .id-lock-free a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-free a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/65/Lock-green.svg")right 0.1em center/9px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .id-lock-limited a,.mw-parser-output .id-lock-registration a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-limited a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-registration a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d6/Lock-gray-alt-2.svg")right 0.1em center/9px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .id-lock-subscription a,.mw-parser-output .citation .cs1-lock-subscription a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/aa/Lock-red-alt-2.svg")right 0.1em center/9px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .cs1-ws-icon a{background:url("//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4c/Wikisource-logo.svg")right 0.1em center/12px no-repeat}.mw-parser-output .cs1-code{color:inherit;background:inherit;border:none;padding:inherit}.mw-parser-output .cs1-hidden-error{display:none;color:#d33}.mw-parser-output .cs1-visible-error{color:#d33}.mw-parser-output .cs1-maint{display:none;color:#3a3;margin-left:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .cs1-format{font-size:95%}.mw-parser-output .cs1-kern-left{padding-left:0.2em}.mw-parser-output .cs1-kern-right{padding-right:0.2em}.mw-parser-output .citation .mw-selflink{font-weight:inherit}</style><cite class="citation cs2"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/stream/chronicleofgreyf00londrich#page/34/mode/2up"><i>Chronicle of the Grey Friars of London</i></a>, 1531</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Chronicle+of+the+Grey+Friars+of+London&amp;rft.date=1531&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Farchive.org%2Fstream%2Fchronicleofgreyf00londrich%23page%2F34%2Fmode%2F2up&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AJohn+Fisher" class="Z3988"></span></cite>
</p>
</div>
<p>A year later, in 1530, the continued encroachments on the Church moved Fisher, as bishop of Rochester, along with the <a href="/wiki/John_Clerk_(bishop)" title="John Clerk (bishop)">bishops of Bath</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_West" title="Nicholas West">Ely</a>, to appeal to the Holy See. This gave the King his opportunity and an edict forbidding such appeals was immediately issued, and the three bishops were arrested. Their imprisonment, however, must have lasted only a few months for in February 1531, <a href="/wiki/Convocations_of_Canterbury_and_York" title="Convocations of Canterbury and York">Convocation</a> met, and Fisher was present. This was the occasion when the clergy were forced, at a cost of 100,000 pounds, to purchase the King's pardon for having recognized <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Wolsey" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Wolsey">Cardinal Wolsey</a>'s authority as legate of the pope; and at the same time to acknowledge Henry as supreme head of the Church in England, to which phrase the addition of the clause "so far as God's law permits" was made through Fisher's efforts.
</p><p>A few days later, several of Fisher's servants were taken ill after eating some porridge served to the household and two died. A cook, <a href="/wiki/Richard_Roose" title="Richard Roose">Richard Roose</a>, was executed by <a href="/wiki/Death_by_boiling" title="Death by boiling">boiling alive</a> for attempted poisoning.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Intrigues_with_the_Holy_Roman_Emperor">Intrigues with the Holy Roman Emperor</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Intrigues with the Holy Roman Emperor">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Fisher also engaged in secret activities to overthrow Henry. As early as 1531 he began secretly communicating with foreign diplomats. In September 1533 communicating secretly through the imperial ambassador <a href="/wiki/Eustace_Chapuys" title="Eustace Chapuys">Eustace Chapuys</a> he encouraged <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a> <a href="/wiki/Charles_V,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Charles V, Holy Roman Emperor">Charles V</a> to invade England and depose Henry in combination with a domestic uprising.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id=".22The_King.27s_Great_Matter.22"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="&quot;The_King's_Great_Matter&quot;">"The King's Great Matter"</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: &quot;The King&#039;s Great Matter&quot;">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/The_King%27s_Great_Matter" class="mw-redirect" title="The King&#39;s Great Matter">The King's Great Matter</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck,_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck%2C_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg/170px-John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck%2C_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="272" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck%2C_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg/255px-John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck%2C_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck%2C_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg/340px-John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck%2C_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:John_Fisher_by_Gerard_Valck,_after_Adriaen_van_der_Werff.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>John Fisher by <a href="/wiki/Gerard_Valck" title="Gerard Valck">Gerard Valck</a>, after <a href="/wiki/Adriaen_van_der_Werff" title="Adriaen van der Werff">Adriaen van der Werff</a>, 1697.</div></div></div>
<p>Matters now moved rapidly.  In May 1532, Sir <a href="/wiki/Thomas_More" title="Thomas More">Thomas More</a> resigned the chancellorship and, in June, Fisher preached publicly against the annulment.  In August, <a href="/wiki/William_Warham" title="William Warham">William Warham</a>, Archbishop of Canterbury, died and <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Cranmer" title="Thomas Cranmer">Thomas Cranmer</a> was at once proposed by Henry to the Pope as his successor.  In January of the next year, Henry secretly went through a form of marriage with <a href="/wiki/Anne_Boleyn" title="Anne Boleyn">Anne Boleyn</a>.  Cranmer's consecration as a bishop took place in March 1533, and, a week later, Fisher was arrested.  It seems that the purpose of this arrest was to prevent him from opposing the annulment which Cranmer pronounced in May, or the coronation of Anne Boleyn which followed on 1 June, for Fisher was set at liberty again within a fortnight of the latter event, no charge being made against him.  In the autumn of 1533, various arrests were made in connection with the so-called revelations of the Holy Maid of Kent, <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_Barton" title="Elizabeth Barton">Elizabeth Barton</a>, but as Fisher was taken seriously ill in December, proceedings against him were postponed for a time.  However, in March 1534, a special <a href="/wiki/Bill_of_Attainder" class="mw-redirect" title="Bill of Attainder">Bill of Attainder</a> against Fisher and others for complicity in the matter of the Maid of Kent was introduced in <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_England" title="Parliament of England">Parliament</a> and passed.  By this, Fisher was condemned to forfeit all his personal estate and to be imprisoned during the King's pleasure.  Subsequently, a pardon was granted him on payment of a fine of 300 pounds.
</p><p>The same session of Parliament passed the <a href="/wiki/First_Succession_Act" title="First Succession Act">First Succession Act</a>, by which all who should be called upon to do so were compelled to take an oath of succession, acknowledging the issue of Henry and Anne as legitimate heirs to the throne, under pain of being guilty of <a href="/wiki/Misprision_of_treason" title="Misprision of treason">misprision of treason</a>.  Fisher refused the oath and was imprisoned in the <a href="/wiki/Tower_of_London" title="Tower of London">Tower of London</a> on 26 April 1534.<sup id="cite_ref-CatholicCulture_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CatholicCulture-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Several efforts were made to induce him to submit, but without effect, and in November he was attained of misprision of treason a second time, his goods being forfeited as from the previous 1 March, and the See of Rochester being declared vacant as of 2 June following.  He was to remain in the Tower for over a year, and while he was allowed food and drink sent by friends, and a servant, he was not allowed a priest, even to the very end.  A long letter exists, written from the Tower by Fisher to <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Cromwell" title="Thomas Cromwell">Thomas Cromwell</a>, speaking of the severity of his conditions of imprisonment.
</p><p>Like Thomas More, Bishop Fisher believed that, because the statute condemned only those speaking maliciously against the King's new title, there was safety in silence. However, on 7 May he fell into a trap laid for him by <a href="/wiki/Richard_Rich,_1st_Baron_Rich" title="Richard Rich, 1st Baron Rich">Richard Rich</a>, who was to perjure himself to obtain Thomas More's conviction. Rich told Fisher that for his own conscience's sake the King wished to know, in strict secrecy, Fisher's real opinion. Fisher, once again, declared that the King was not Supreme Head of the Church of England.<sup id="cite_ref-foley_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foley-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cardinalate_and_martyrdom">Cardinalate and martyrdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Cardinalate and martyrdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG/220px-London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG/330px-London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG/440px-London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2700" data-file-height="1800" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:London_01_2013_Tower_Hill_scaffold_5211.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Memorial space at the <a href="/wiki/Tower_Hill" title="Tower Hill">Tower Hill</a> public execution site</div></div></div>
<p>In May 1535, the newly elected <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_III" title="Pope Paul III">Pope Paul III</a> created Fisher <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal Priest">Cardinal Priest</a> of <i><a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_San_Vitale_(Rome)" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of San Vitale (Rome)">San Vitale</a></i>, apparently in the hope of inducing Henry to ease Fisher's treatment. The effect was precisely the reverse:<sup id="cite_ref-foley_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foley-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Henry forbade the cardinal's hat to be brought into England, declaring that he would send the head to Rome instead. In June a special commission for Fisher's trial was issued, and on Thursday, 17 June, he was arraigned in <a href="/wiki/Westminster_Hall" class="mw-redirect" title="Westminster Hall">Westminster Hall</a> before a court of seventeen, including <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Cromwell,_1st_Earl_of_Essex" class="mw-redirect" title="Thomas Cromwell, 1st Earl of Essex">Thomas Cromwell</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anne_Boleyn" title="Anne Boleyn">Anne Boleyn</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Boleyn,_1st_Earl_of_Wiltshire" title="Thomas Boleyn, 1st Earl of Wiltshire">father</a>, and ten justices. The charge was <a href="/wiki/Treason" title="Treason">treason</a>, in that he denied that the King was the Supreme Head of the Church of England. Since he had been deprived of his position of <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Rochester" title="Bishop of Rochester">Bishop of Rochester</a> by the <a href="/wiki/Act_of_Attainder" class="mw-redirect" title="Act of Attainder">Act of Attainder</a>, he was treated as a commoner, and tried by jury. The only testimony was that of Richard Rich. John Fisher was found guilty and condemned to be <a href="/wiki/Hanged,_drawn_and_quartered" title="Hanged, drawn and quartered">hanged, drawn and quartered</a> at <a href="/wiki/Tyburn" title="Tyburn">Tyburn</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG/220px-London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="221" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG/330px-London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG/440px-London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2364" data-file-height="2371" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:London_bell_tower_08.03.2013_12-32-17.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Bell Tower, where John Fisher was held during his prison time together with <a href="/wiki/Thomas_More" title="Thomas More">Thomas More</a>, though imprisoned separately therein.</div></div></div>
<p>However, a public outcry was brewing among the London populace who saw a sinister irony in the parallels between the conviction of Fisher and that of his patronal namesake, <a href="/wiki/John_the_Baptist" title="John the Baptist">Saint John the Baptist</a>, who was executed by King <a href="/wiki/Herod_Antipas" title="Herod Antipas">Herod Antipas</a> for challenging the validity of Herod's marriage to his brother's divorcée <a href="/wiki/Herodias" title="Herodias">Herodias</a>. For fear of John Fisher's living through his patronal <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a>, that of the <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_St._John_the_Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Nativity of St. John the Baptist">Nativity of St. John the Baptist</a> on 24 June, and of attracting too much public sympathy, King Henry commuted the sentence to that of <a href="/wiki/Decapitation" title="Decapitation">beheading</a>, to be accomplished before 23 June, the Vigil of the feast of the Nativity of St. John the Baptist. He was executed on <a href="/wiki/Tower_Hill" title="Tower Hill">Tower Hill</a> on 22 June 1535.<sup id="cite_ref-FULLER_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FULLER-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The execution had the opposite effect from that which King Henry VIII intended, as it created yet another parallel with that of the martyrdom of St. John the Baptist, who was also beheaded; his death also happened on the feast day of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Alban" title="Saint Alban">Saint Alban</a>, the first martyr of Britain.<sup id="cite_ref-FULLER_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FULLER-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Fisher's last moments were in keeping with his life. He met death with a calm dignified courage which profoundly impressed those present. His body was treated with particular rancour, apparently on Henry's orders, being stripped and left on the scaffold until the evening,<sup id="cite_ref-foley_6-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-foley-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> when it was taken on pikes and thrown naked into a rough grave in the churchyard of All Hallows' Barking, also known as <a href="/wiki/All_Hallows-by-the-Tower" title="All Hallows-by-the-Tower">All Hallows-by-the-Tower</a>. There was no funeral prayer. A fortnight later, his body was laid beside that of Sir <a href="/wiki/Thomas_More" title="Thomas More">Thomas More</a> in the chapel of <a href="/wiki/Church_of_St_Peter_ad_Vincula" title="Church of St Peter ad Vincula">St Peter ad Vincula</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Tower_of_London" title="Tower of London">Tower of London</a>. Fisher's head was <a href="/wiki/Head_on_a_spike" title="Head on a spike">stuck upon a pole</a> on <a href="/wiki/London_Bridge" title="London Bridge">London Bridge</a> but its ruddy and lifelike appearance excited so much attention that, after a fortnight, it was thrown into the <a href="/wiki/Thames" class="mw-redirect" title="Thames">Thames</a>, its place being taken by that of Sir Thomas More, whose execution, also at Tower Hill, occurred on 6 July.<sup id="cite_ref-huddleston_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-huddleston-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonisation">Canonisation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Canonisation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Fisher was <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a> with <a href="/wiki/Thomas_More" title="Thomas More">Thomas More</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_Catholic_martyrs_of_the_English_Reformation#Beatified_29_December_1886_by_Pope_Leo_XIII" title="List of Catholic martyrs of the English Reformation">52 other English Martyrs</a> on 29 December 1886. In the Decree of Beatification, the greatest place was given to Fisher.
</p><p>He was <a href="/wiki/Canonise" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonise">canonised</a>, with Thomas More, on 19 May 1935 by Pope Pius XI, after the presentation of a petition by English Catholics.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> His feast day, for celebration jointly with St Thomas More, is on 22 June (the date of Fisher's execution). In 1980, despite being an opponent of the <a href="/wiki/English_Reformation" title="English Reformation">English Reformation</a>, Fisher was added to the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England's</a> <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">calendar of Saints and Heroes of the Christian Church</a>, jointly with Thomas More, to be <a href="/wiki/Commemoration_(Anglicanism)" title="Commemoration (Anglicanism)">commemorated</a> every <a href="/wiki/6_July" class="mw-redirect" title="6 July">6 July</a><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> (the date of More's execution) as "Thomas More, Scholar, and John Fisher, Bishop of Rochester, Reformation Martyrs, 1535".<sup id="cite_ref-CofEholyDays_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CofEholyDays-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> He is also listed along with <a href="/wiki/Thomas_More" title="Thomas More">Thomas More</a> in the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Anglican_Church_calendars#Calendars_of_saints&#39;_days_in_churches_throughout_the_Anglican_communion" title="List of Anglican Church calendars">calendar of saints</a> of some of the other Churches of the <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican Communion</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Anglican_Church_of_Australia)#July" title="Calendar of saints (Anglican Church of Australia)">The Anglican Church of Australia</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Portraits">Portraits</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Portraits">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Several portraits of Fisher exist, the most prominent being by <a href="/wiki/Hans_Holbein_the_Younger" title="Hans Holbein the Younger">Hans Holbein the Younger</a> in the Royal Collection; and a few <a href="/wiki/Secondary_relic" class="mw-redirect" title="Secondary relic">secondary relics</a> are extant.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Relic">Relic</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Relic">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Fisher's walking-staff is in the possession of the Eyston family of <a href="/wiki/East_Hendred" title="East Hendred">East Hendred</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Oxfordshire" title="Oxfordshire">Oxfordshire</a> (formerly <a href="/wiki/Berkshire" title="Berkshire">Berkshire</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Cinematic_and_television_portrayals">Cinematic and television portrayals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Cinematic and television portrayals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>John Fisher was portrayed by veteran actor <a href="/wiki/Joseph_O%27Conor" title="Joseph O&#39;Conor">Joseph O'Conor</a> in the film <i><a href="/wiki/Anne_of_the_Thousand_Days" title="Anne of the Thousand Days">Anne of the Thousand Days</a></i> (1969), by <a href="/wiki/Bosco_Hogan" title="Bosco Hogan">Bosco Hogan</a> in the miniseries <i><a href="/wiki/The_Tudors" title="The Tudors">The Tudors</a></i>, by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Geoffrey_Lewis_(British_actor)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Geoffrey Lewis (British actor) (page does not exist)">Geoffrey Lewis</a> in the 1971 miniseries <i><a href="/wiki/The_Six_Wives_of_Henry_VIII_(BBC_TV_series)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Six Wives of Henry VIII (BBC TV series)">The Six Wives of Henry VIII</a></i> and by <a href="/wiki/Richard_Durden" title="Richard Durden">Richard Durden</a> in the 2015 miniseries <i><a href="/wiki/Wolf_Hall_(miniseries)" class="mw-redirect" title="Wolf Hall (miniseries)">Wolf Hall</a></i>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Writings">Writings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Writings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>A list of Fisher's writings is found in <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Gillow" title="Joseph Gillow">Joseph Gillow</a>'s <i>Bibliographical Dictionary of the English Catholics</i> (London, s.d.), II, 262–270. There are twenty-six works in all, printed and manuscript, mostly ascetical or controversial treatises, several of which have been reprinted many times. The original editions are very rare and valuable. The principal are:
</p>
<ul><li><span title="Middle English (1100-1500)-language text"><i lang="enm">Treatise concernynge ... the seven penytencyall Psalms</i></span> (London, 1508)</li>
<li><span title="Middle English (1100-1500)-language text"><i lang="enm">Sermon ... agayn ye pernicyous doctrin of Martin Luther</i></span> (London, 1521)</li>
<li><span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la">Defensio Henrici VIII</i></span> (Cologne, 1525)</li>
<li><span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la">De Veritate Corporis et Sanguinis Christi in Eucharistia, adversus Johannem Oecolampadium</i></span> (Cologne, 1527)</li>
<li><span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la">De Causa Matrimonii ... Henrici VIII cum Catharina Aragonensi</i></span> (Alcalá de Henares, 1530)</li>
<li><span title="Middle English (1100-1500)-language text"><i lang="enm">The Wayes to Perfect Religion</i></span> (London, 1535)</li>
<li><span title="Middle English (1100-1500)-language text"><i lang="enm">A Spirituall Consolation written ... to hys sister Elizabeth</i></span> (London, 1735)</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Patronage">Patronage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Patronage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Australia">Australia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Australia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_College_(University_of_Tasmania)" class="mw-redirect" title="St John Fisher College (University of Tasmania)">St John Fisher College</a> at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Tasmania" title="University of Tasmania">University of Tasmania</a> in <a href="/wiki/Hobart" title="Hobart">Hobart</a></li>
<li>St John Fisher Catholic High school in <a href="/wiki/Bracken_Ridge,_Queensland" title="Bracken Ridge, Queensland">Bracken Ridge, Queensland</a><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mary_Immaculate_Church,_Annerley" title="Mary Immaculate Church, Annerley">St John Fisher Church</a> (Tarragindi), Brisbane, Queensland<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canada">Canada</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Canada">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>John Fisher Public School, Toronto</li>
<li>Fisher Hall, one of the residence halls at Saint Michael's College at the University of Toronto</li>
<li>St. John Fisher R.C. School, Forest, Ontario<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>St. John Fisher Parish, Bramalea (Brampton), Ontario<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>St. John Fisher Elementary School, Pointe-Claire, Quebec<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="United_Kingdom">United Kingdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: United Kingdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cambridge" title="University of Cambridge">University of Cambridge</a>:
<ul><li>Fisher House, the <a href="/wiki/Cambridge_University_Catholic_Chaplaincy" title="Cambridge University Catholic Chaplaincy">Cambridge University Catholic Chaplaincy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Fisher Building, a student accommodation at <a href="/wiki/Queens%27_College,_Cambridge" title="Queens&#39; College, Cambridge">Queens' College</a>, where Fisher was president from 1505 to 1508<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The Fisher Building, conference centre and meeting rooms in <a href="/wiki/St_John%27s_College,_Cambridge" title="St John&#39;s College, Cambridge">St John's College</a><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li>
<li>Schools:
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_Catholic_College" title="St John Fisher Catholic College">St John Fisher Catholic College</a> in <a href="/wiki/Newcastle-under-Lyme" title="Newcastle-under-Lyme">Newcastle-under-Lyme</a>, <a href="/wiki/Staffordshire" title="Staffordshire">Staffordshire</a><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_Catholic_High_School,_Harrogate" title="St John Fisher Catholic High School, Harrogate">St John Fisher Catholic High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/Harrogate" title="Harrogate">Harrogate</a>, North Yorkshire<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_Catholic_High_School,_Peterborough" title="St John Fisher Catholic High School, Peterborough">St John Fisher Catholic High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/Peterborough" title="Peterborough">Peterborough</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cambridgeshire" title="Cambridgeshire">Cambridgeshire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_Catholic_High_School,_Wigan" title="St John Fisher Catholic High School, Wigan">St John Fisher Catholic High School</a>, Wigan, Lancashire<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_Catholic_School" title="St John Fisher Catholic School">St John Fisher Catholic School</a> in Chatham, Kent.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_John_Fisher_Catholic_Voluntary_Academy" title="St John Fisher Catholic Voluntary Academy">St John Fisher Catholic Voluntary Academy</a> in <a href="/wiki/Dewsbury" title="Dewsbury">Dewsbury</a>, West Yorkshire<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ss_John_Fisher_and_Thomas_More_Roman_Catholic_High_School" title="Ss John Fisher and Thomas More Roman Catholic High School">Ss John Fisher and Thomas More Roman Catholic High School</a>, Colne, Lancashire<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/The_John_Fisher_School" title="The John Fisher School">The John Fisher School</a> located in <a href="/wiki/Purley,_London" title="Purley, London">Purley</a>, <a href="/wiki/Surrey" title="Surrey">Surrey</a><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li>
<li>Churches:
<ul><li>Saint John Fisher parish, Kidbrooke, London<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Saint John Fisher Roman Catholic Church, Harrow, London</li>
<li>Saint John Fisher Roman Catholic Church, Shepperton, London</li>
<li>Saint John Fisher Roman Catholic Church, Rochester, Kent</li>
<li>John Fisher R.C Church, Scarthoe, Great Grimsby, Lincolnshire. (Closed 2017)</li>
<li>Saint John Fisher R.C. Church, Priory Crescent, Southend on Sea, Essex</li>
<li>Saint John Fisher Church, <a href="/wiki/Cambourne" title="Cambourne">Cambourne</a>, Cambridgeshire</li></ul></li>
<li>Other:
<ul><li>St John Fisher House, <a href="/wiki/Reading,_Berkshire" title="Reading, Berkshire">Reading</a> – the headquarters of the <a href="/wiki/Priestly_Fraternity_of_St._Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="Priestly Fraternity of St. Peter">FSSP</a> in <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wales" title="Wales">Wales</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The football teams <a href="/wiki/Fisher_Athletic_F.C." title="Fisher Athletic F.C.">Fisher Athletic F.C.</a> (1908–2009) and <a href="/wiki/Fisher_F.C." title="Fisher F.C.">Fisher F.C.</a> (founded 2009) of <a href="/wiki/Rotherhithe" title="Rotherhithe">Rotherhithe</a>, south-east London<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="United_States">United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Fisher&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Due to his status as the Bishop of Rochester, Fisher has been adopted as a patron of several institutions in other cities named Rochester, including <a href="/wiki/St._John_Fisher_University" title="St. John Fisher University">St. John Fisher University</a><sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> and Saint John of Rochester Catholic Church in the <a href="/wiki/Rochester,_New_York" title="Rochester, New York">Rochester, New York</a> area, and Saint John Fisher Catholic Church near Rochester, Michigan. Fisher is also the patron of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Rochester" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Rochester">Roman Catholic Diocese of Rochester</a> in New York State, so named by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_XXIII" title="Pope John XXIII">Pope John XXIII</a> in 1961.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>A number of parishes are dedicated to St. John Fisher including those in: Chicago, Illinois;<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Auburn Hills, Michigan;<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Cincinnati, Ohio;<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Boothwyn, Pennsylvania,<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Galveston, Texas,<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Rancho_Palos_Verdes" class="mw-redirect" title="Rancho Palos Verdes">Rancho Palos Verdes</a>, California,<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> and Portland, Oregon,<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._John_Fisher_Seminary_Residence" title="St. John Fisher Seminary Residence">St. John Fisher Seminary Residence</a> is located in the Catholic Diocese of Bridgeport, Connecticut.</li>
<li>St John Fisher Catholic Church in <a href="/wiki/Tulsa" class="mw-redirect" title="Tulsa">Tulsa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oklahoma" title="Oklahoma">Oklahoma</a> and one near <a href="/wiki/Pittsburgh" title="Pittsburgh">Pittsburgh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pennsylvania" title="Pennsylvania">Pennsylvania</a>.</li>
<li>Saint John Fisher Church, Newtown, Ohio</li>
<li>St John Fisher Catholic Church in <a href="/wiki/Marlborough,_Connecticut" title="Marlborough, Connecticut">Marlborough, Connecticut</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
